L /// Asserts that an expression matches the provided pattern.
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 60 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L ///
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
L /// This macro is generally preferable to `assert!(matches!(value, pattern))`, because it can print
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 99 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L /// the debug representation of the actual value shape that did not meet expectations. In contrast,
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 99 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L /// using [`assert!`] will only print that expectations were not met, but not why.
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 82 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L ///
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
L /// The pattern syntax is exactly the same as found in a match arm and the `matches!` macro. The
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 96 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L /// optional if guard can be used to add additional checks that must be true for the matched value,
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 99 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L /// otherwise this macro will panic.
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 36 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L ///
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
L /// Assertions are always checked in both debug and release builds, and cannot
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust

T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 10 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L ///
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
L /// Unlike [`assert!`], `debug_assert!` statements are only enabled in non
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 74 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L /// optimized builds by default. An optimized build will not execute
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 68 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L /// `debug_assert!` statements unless `-C debug-assertions` is passed to the
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 76 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L /// compiler. This makes `debug_assert!` useful for checks that are too
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 71 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L /// expensive to be present in a release build but may be helpful during
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 72 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L /// development. The result of expanding `debug_assert!` is always type checked.
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 80 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L ///
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
L /// An unchecked assertion allows a program in an inconsistent state to keep
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 76 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L /// running, which might have unexpected consequences but does not introduce
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 76 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L /// unsafety as long as this only happens in safe code. The performance cost
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 76 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L /// of assertions, however, is not measurable in general. Replacing [`assert!`]
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 79 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L /// with `debug_assert!` is thus only encouraged after thorough profiling, and
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
T 78 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust
L /// more importantly, only in safe code!
T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust

T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust

T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust

T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust

T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust

T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust

T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust

T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust

T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust

T 3 source.rust comment.line.documentation.rust punctuation.definition.comment.rust
